````math
r^{\star}(x,y):=\log\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid{}x)=\beta\log\left(\frac{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid{}x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid{}x)}\right)+Z(x),
``` |  | (164) |

and let
$`\widehat{r}(x,y)\vcentcolon={}\beta\log\left(\frac{\widehat{\pi}(y\mid{}x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid{}x)}\right)`$.
By a standard argument ([Huang et al., 2024](#bib.bib51)), we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}\in\argmax_{\pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})}\En_{\pi}[\widehat{r}(x,y)]-\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right).`$ |  | (165) |

Therefore for any comparator model
$`\pi^{\star}:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})`$ (not necessarily in
the model class $`\Pi`$), we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\En_{\pi^{\star}}[r^{\star}(x,y)]-\En_{\widehat{\pi}}[r^{\star}(x,y)]`$ |  | (166) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\En_{\pi^{\star}}[\widehat{r}(x,y)]-\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)-\En_{\widehat{\pi}}[\widehat{r}(x,y)]+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)`$ |  | (167) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\En_{\pi^{\star}}[r^{\star}(x,y)-\widehat{r}(x,y)]+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)+\En_{\widehat{\pi}}[\widehat{r}(x,y)-r^{\star}(x,y)]-\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)`$ |  | (168) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\En_{\pi^{\star}}[r^{\star}(x,y)-\widehat{r}(x,y)]+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)+\En_{\widehat{\pi}}[\widehat{r}(x,y)-r^{\star}(x,y)]-\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)`$ |  | (169) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\En_{\pi^{\star},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right]+\En_{\widehat{\pi},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right]`$ |  | (170) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)-\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)`$ |  | (171) |

where the inequality uses
[Eq. 165](#A10.E165 "In J.1.2 Proof of ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
To bound the right-hand side above, we will use the following lemma,
which is proven in the sequel. {lemma} For any model $`\pi`$ and any
$`\eta>0`$, we have that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\En_{\pi,\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert\right]`$ |  | (172) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}\mathcal{C}_{\pi}^{1/2}\cdot\left(\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{2}\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert\leq\eta,\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert\leq\eta\right\}\right]\right)^{1/2}`$ |  | (173) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~+\mathcal{C}_{\pi}^{1/2}(\log(\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\pi_{\texttt{base}}}}{{\widehat{\pi}}};\beta})+\log(\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\pi_{\texttt{base}}}}{{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}};\beta}))\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]\right)^{1/4}.`$ |  | (174) |

Using
[Section J.1.2](#A10.SS1.SSS2 "J.1.2 Proof of ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
to bound the first two terms of
[Eq. 171](#A10.E171 "In J.1.2 Proof of ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
and using the fact that all $`\pi\in\Pi`$ have
$`\mathcal{C}_{\pi}\leq C_{\texttt{conc}}`$ and
$`\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\pi_{\texttt{base}}}}{{\pi}};\beta}\leq C_{\texttt{loss}}`$,
we have that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi})`$ |  | (175) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}(\mathcal{C}_{\pi^{\star}}+C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\cdot\left(\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{2}\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert\leq\eta,\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert\leq\eta\right\}\right]\right)^{1/2}`$ |  | (176) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+(\mathcal{C}_{\pi^{\star}}+C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\log(C_{\texttt{loss}})\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]\right)^{1/4}+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right).`$ |  | (177) |

Let us overload notation and write
$`\Delta^{\pi}(x,y,y^{\prime})=\beta\log\left(\frac{\pi(y\mid{}x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid{}x)}\right)-\beta\log\left(\frac{\pi(y^{\prime}\mid{}x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid{}x)}\right)`$,
so that $`\Delta^{\widehat{\pi}}=\Delta^{\widehat{r}}`$ and
$`\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}=\Delta^{r^{\star}}`$. Since
$`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\in\Pi`$, the definition of $`\widehat{\pi}`$ in
[Eq. 7](#S2.E7 "In 2.2 Self-Improvement through RLHF: RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 2 Sharpening Algorithms for Self-Improvement ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
implies that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{\texttt{pref}}}\left(\Delta^{\widehat{\pi}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq{}\min_{\pi\in\Pi}\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{\texttt{pref}}}\left(\Delta^{\pi}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}`$ |  | (178) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq{}\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{\texttt{pref}}}\left(\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}`$ |  | (179) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=0.`$ |  | (180) |

Define
$`B_{n,\rho}:=\log(2nC_{\texttt{loss}}\lvert\Pi\rvert\rho^{-1})`$. It is
immediate that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}_{\texttt{pref}}}\left(\Delta^{\widehat{\pi}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{\pi}}\big\rvert\leq B_{n,\rho},\big\lvert\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}\big\rvert\leq B_{n,\rho}\right\}\leq{}0.`$ |  | (181) |

From here, Bernstein’s inequality and a union bound implies that with
probability at least $`1-\rho`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{\pi}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{2}\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{\pi}}\big\rvert\leq B_{n,\rho},\big\lvert\Delta^{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}\big\rvert\leq B_{n,\rho}\right\}\right]`$ |  | (182) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}\frac{B_{n,\rho}^{2}\log(\lvert\Pi\rvert\rho^{-1})}{n}=\vcentcolon\varepsilon_{\texttt{stat}}^{2}.`$ |  | (183) |

In particular, if we combine this with
[Eq. 177](#A10.E177 "In J.1.2 Proof of ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
and set $`\eta=B_{n,\rho}`$, then
[Section J.1.1](#A10.SS1.SSS1 "J.1.1 Technical lemmas ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
implies that

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}(\mathcal{C}_{\pi^{\star}}+C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\texttt{stat}}+(\mathcal{C}_{\pi^{\star}}+C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\log(C_{\texttt{loss}})\cdot\rho^{1/4}+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right).`$ |  | (184) |

Note that the above bound holds for any
$`\pi^{\star}:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})`$. We define
$`\pi^{\star}`$ by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi^{\star}(y\mid x):=\frac{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)\mathbb{I}[y\in\bm{y}^{\star}_{\gamma}(x)]}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(\bm{y}^{\star}_{\gamma}(x)\mid x)},
``` |  | (185) |

which can be seen to satisfy
$`\mathcal{C}_{\pi^{\star}}\leq{}C_{\texttt{cov},\gamma}\leq C_{\texttt{conc}}`$
and
$`D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\pi^{\star}\,\|\,\pi_{\texttt{base}}\right)\leq\log(\mathcal{C}_{\pi^{\star}})\leq\log(C_{\texttt{conc}})`$.
With this choice, we can further bound the expression above by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}(C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\texttt{stat}}+(C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\log(C_{\texttt{loss}})\cdot\rho^{1/4}+\beta\log(C_{\texttt{conc}})`$ |  | (186) |

Given a desired failure probability $`\rho`$, applying the bound above
with
$`\rho^{\prime}\vcentcolon={}\rho\wedge{}(\varepsilon_{\texttt{stat}}/\log(C_{\texttt{loss}}))^{4}`$
then gives

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}(C_{\texttt{conc}})^{1/2}\cdot\varepsilon_{\texttt{stat}}+\beta\log(C_{\texttt{conc}}).`$ |  | (187) |

Finally, we observe that for our choice of $`\pi^{\star}`$, under the
margin condition with parameter $`\gamma`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J(\pi^{\star})-J(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\En_{x\sim\mu}\En_{y,y^{\prime}\sim\pi^{\star},\widehat{\pi}}\left[\log\left(\frac{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid{}x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid{}x)}\right)\right]`$ |  | (188) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\gtrsim{}\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}\cdot\En_{x\sim\mu}\En_{y^{\prime}\sim\widehat{\pi}}\left[\mathbb{I}\{y^{\prime}\not\in\bm{y}^{\star}_{\gamma}(x)\}\right]-\gamma`$ |  | (189) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\gtrsim{}\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}\delta\cdot\En_{x\sim\mu}\left[\mathbb{I}\{\widehat{\pi}(\bm{y}^{\star}_{\gamma}(x)\mid{}x)\leq{}1-\delta\}\right]-\gamma`$ |  | (190) |

where the first inequality uses
[Section J.1](#A10.SS1 "J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
together with the fact that $`y\in\bm{y}^{\star}_{\gamma}(x)`$ with
probability $`1`$ over $`x\sim\mu`$ and
$`y\sim\pi^{\star}(\cdot\mid x)`$. This proves the result.

∎

Proof of
[Section J.1.2](#A10.SS1.SSS2 "J.1.2 Proof of ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
For any $`\eta>0`$, we can bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\En_{\pi,\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq{}\En_{\pi,\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert\leq\eta,\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert\leq\eta\right\}\right]`$ |  | (191) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle~~~~+\En_{\pi,\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\vee\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right\}\right].`$ |  | (192) |

For the second term above, we can use Cauchy-Schwarz to bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\En_{\pi,\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\vee\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right\}\right]`$ |  | (193) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq{}\mathcal{C}_{\pi}^{1/2}\cdot\left(\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{2}\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\vee\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right\}\right]\right)^{1/2}`$ |  | (194) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}\mathcal{C}_{\pi}^{1/2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]\right)^{1/4}`$ |  | (195) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot{}\left(\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{4}\right]+\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{4}\right]\right)^{1/4}`$ |  | (196) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim{}\mathcal{C}_{\pi}^{1/2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert>\eta\right]\right)^{1/4}\cdot{}(\log(\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\pi_{\texttt{base}}}}{{\widehat{\pi}}};\beta})+\log(\mathcal{C}_{\nicefrac{{\pi_{\texttt{base}}}}{{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}};\beta})),`$ |  | (197) |

where the last inequality follows from
[Section J.1.1](#A10.SS1.SSS1 "J.1.1 Technical lemmas ‣ J.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").

Meanwhile, for the first term, for any $`\lambda>0`$ we can bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\En_{\pi,\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert\leq\eta,\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert\leq\eta\right\}\right]`$ |  | (198) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq{}\mathcal{C}_{\pi}^{1/2}\left(\En_{\pi_{\texttt{base}},\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\left[\left\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}(x,y,y^{\prime})-\Delta^{\widehat{r}}(x,y,y^{\prime})\right\rvert^{2}\mathbb{I}\left\{\big\lvert\Delta^{r^{\star}}\big\rvert\leq\eta,\big\lvert\Delta^{\widehat{r}}\big\rvert\leq\eta\right\}\right]\right)^{1/2}.`$ |  | (199) |

∎

#### J.2 Proof of [Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px1 "Main result ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism") and [Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px2 "Example: Linearly parameterized models ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")

In this section we prove
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px1 "Main result ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
as well as
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px2 "Example: Linearly parameterized models ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
the application to linear softmax models. For the formal theorem
statements, see
[Section J.2.3](#A10.SS2.SSS3 "J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
and
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
respectively. The section is organized as follows.

- •
  [Section J.2.1](#A10.SS2.SSS1 "J.2.1 Background ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
  gives necessary background on KL-regularized policy optimization, as
  well as the Sequential Extrapolation Coefficient.
- •
  [Section J.2.2](#A10.SS2.SSS2 "J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
  presents a generic guarantee for XPO under a general choice of reward
  function.
- •
  [Section J.2.3](#A10.SS2.SSS3 "J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
  instantiates the result above with the self-reward function
  $`r(x,y):=\log\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)`$ to prove
  [Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px1 "Main result ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
- •
  Finally,
  [Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
  applies the preceding results to prove
  [Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px2 "Example: Linearly parameterized models ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").

##### J.2.1 Background

To begin, we give background on KL-regularized policy optimization and
the Sequential Extrapolation Coefficient.

###### KL-regularized policy optimization

Let $`\beta>0`$ be given, and let
$`r:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to[-R_{\mathsf{max}},R_{\mathsf{max}}]`$
be an unknown reward function on prompt/action pairs. Define a value
function $`J_{\beta}`$ over model class $`\Pi`$ by:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J_{\beta}(\pi):=\En_{\pi}[r(x,y)]-\beta\cdot D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\mathbb{P}^{\pi}\,\|\,\mathbb{P}^{\pi_{\texttt{base}}}\right).`$ |  | (200) |

We refer to this as a *KL-regularized policy optimization* objective (we
use the term “policy” following the reinforcement learning literature;
for our setting, policies correspond to models). Given query access to
$`r`$, the goal is to find $`\widehat{\pi}\in\Pi`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-J_{\beta}(\widehat{\pi})\leq\epsilon`$ |  | (201) |

where
$`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)\propto\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)\exp(\beta^{-1}r(x,y))`$
is the model that maximizes $`J_{\beta}`$ over all models
$`\pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})`$.

We make use of the following assumptions, as in [Xie et al.
(2024)](#bib.bib108).

{assumption}

\[Realizability\] It holds that $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\in\Pi`$.

{assumption}

\[Bounded density ratios\] For all $`\pi\in\Pi`$,
$`(x,y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}`$,
$`\big\lvert\beta\log\frac{\pi(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}\big\rvert\leq V_{\mathsf{max}}`$.

Finally, we require two definitions.

{definition}

\[Sequential Extrapolation Coefficient for RLHF, ([Xie et al.,
2024](#bib.bib108))\] For a model class $`\Pi`$, reward function $`r`$,
reference model $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$, and parameters
$`T\in\mathbb{N}`$ and $`\beta,\lambda>0`$, the Sequential Extrapolation
Coefficient is defined as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi,r,T,\beta,\lambda;\pi_{\texttt{base}})`$ |  | (202) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle:=\sup_{\pi^{(1)},\dots,\pi^{(T)}\in\Pi}\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathbb{E}^{(t)}\left[\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right]^{2}}{\lambda\lor\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}^{(i)}\left[\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]}\right\}`$ |  | (203) |

where $`\mathbb{E}^{(t)}`$ denotes expectation over $`x\sim\mu`$,
$`y\sim\pi^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x)`$, and
$`y^{\prime}\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}(\cdot\mid x)`$.

{definition}

Let $`\epsilon>0`$. We say that $`\Psi\subseteq\Pi`$ is a
$`\epsilon`$-net for model class $`\Pi`$ if for every $`\pi\in\Pi`$
there exists $`\pi^{\prime}\in\Psi`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\max_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\max_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\left|\log\frac{\pi(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\prime}(y\mid x)}\right|\leq\epsilon.
``` |  | (204) |

We write $`\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon)`$ to denote the size of the
smallest $`\epsilon`$-net for $`\Pi`$.

##### J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO

input: Base model
$`\pi_{\texttt{base}}:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})`$, reward
function $`r:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}`$, number of
iterations $`T\in\mathbb{N}`$, KL regularization coefficient
$`\beta>0`$, optimism coefficient $`\alpha>0`$.

Initialize: $`\pi^{(1)}\leftarrow\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$,
$`\mathcal{D}^{(0)}\leftarrow\varnothing`$.

for iteration $`t=1,\dots,T`$ do

  Generate sample: $`(x^{(t)},y^{(t)},\widetilde{y}^{(t)})`$ via
$`x^{(t)}\sim\mu`$, $`y^{(t)}\sim\pi^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x^{(t)})`$,
$`\widetilde{y}^{(t)}\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}(\cdot\mid x^{(t)})`$.

  Update dataset:
$`\mathcal{D}^{(t)}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}^{(t-1)}\cup\{(x^{(t)},y^{(t)},\widetilde{y}^{(t)})\}`$.

  Model optimization with global optimism:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi^{(t+1)}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leftarrow\argmin_{\pi\in\Pi}\Bigg\{\alpha\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}^{(t)}}\log(\pi(y^{\prime}\mid x))`$ |  | (205) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}^{(t)}}\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}-\beta\log\frac{\pi(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}-(r(x,y)-r(x,y^{\prime}))\right)^{2}\Bigg\}.`$ |  | (206) |

return:
$`\widehat{\pi}\leftarrow\argmax_{t\in[T+1]}J_{\beta}(\pi^{(t)})`$.
$`\triangleright`$ Can estimate $`J_{\beta}(\pi^{(t)})`$ using
validation data.

Algorithm 1 Reward-based variant of Exploratory Preference Optimization
([Xie et al., 2024](#bib.bib108))

In this section, we give self-contained guarantees for the XPO algorithm
([Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")).
XPO was introduced in [Xie et al. (2024)](#bib.bib108) for
KL-regularized policy optimization in the related setting where the
learner only has indirect access to the reward function $`r`$ through
*preference data* (specifically, pairs of actions labeled via a
Bradley-Terry model). Standard offline algorithms for this problem, such
as DPO, require bounds on concentrability of the model class (see e.g.
[Eq. 22](#S4.E22 "In Assumptions ‣ 4.2.1 Guarantees for RLHF-Sharpening with Direct Preference Optimization ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")).
[Xie et al. (2024)](#bib.bib108) show that the XPO algorithm avoids this
dependence, and instead requires bounded Sequential Extrapolation
Coefficient.

[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
is a variant of the XPO algorithm which is adapted to reward-based
feedback (as opposed to preference-based feedback), and
[Section J.2.2](#A10.SS2.SSS2 "J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
shows that this algorithm enjoys guarantees similar to those of [Xie
et al. (2024)](#bib.bib108) for this setting. Note that this is not an
immediate corollary of the results in [Xie et al. (2024)](#bib.bib108),
since the sample complexity in the preference-based setting scales with
$`e^{O(R_{\mathsf{max}})}`$, and for our application to sharpening it is
important to avoid this dependence. However, our algorithm and analysis
only diverge from [Xie et al. (2024)](#bib.bib108) in a few places.

{theorem}

\[Variant of [Xie et al. (2024, Theorem 3.1)](#bib.bib108)\] Suppose
that
[Sections J.2.1](#A10.SS2.SSS1.Px1 "KL-regularized policy optimization ‣ J.2.1 Background ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
and [J.2.1](#A10.SS2.SSS1.Px1 "KL-regularized policy optimization ‣ J.2.1 Background ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
hold. For any $`T\in\mathbb{N}`$,
$`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}},\rho\in(0,1)`$, by setting
$`\alpha:=\frac{\beta}{R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})T/\rho)}{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}}`$,
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
produces a model $`\widehat{\pi}\in\Pi`$ such that with probability at
least $`1-\rho`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right)=J_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-J_{\beta}(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim(R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}})\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)\log(2\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})T/\rho)}{T}}`$ |  | (207) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\sqrt{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}`$ |  | (208) |

where
$`\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi):=\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi,r,T,\beta,V_{\mathsf{max}}^{2};\pi_{\texttt{base}})`$.

Proof of
[Section J.2.2](#A10.SS2.SSS2 "J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
For compactness, we abbreviate
$`\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi):=\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi,r,T,\beta,V_{\mathsf{max}}^{2};\pi_{\texttt{base}})`$.
From Equation (37) of [Xie et al. (2024)](#bib.bib108), we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}J_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-J_{\beta}(\pi^{(t)})`$ |  | (209) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha}{\beta}(R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}})^{2}\cdot\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)+\frac{\beta}{\alpha T}+\frac{V_{\mathsf{max}}}{T}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\E_{(x,y)\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}}[\beta\log\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)-\beta\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)]`$ |  | (210) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}})^{2}T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\sim\mu\\
y,y^{\prime}\sim{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}^{(t)}\mid x\end{subarray}}\left[\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]`$ |  | (211) |

where
$`{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}^{(t)}:=\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{i<t}\pi^{(i)}\otimes\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$
denotes the model that, given $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$, samples
$`i\sim\mathsf{Unif}([t-1])`$ and then samples
$`y\sim\pi^{(i)}(\cdot\mid x)`$ and
$`y^{\prime}\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}(\cdot\mid x)`$. For any
$`2\leq t\leq T`$, define $`L^{(t)}:\Pi\to[0,\infty)`$ by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle L^{(t)}(\pi)`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\E_{(x,y)\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}}[\beta\log\pi(y\mid x)-\beta\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)]`$ |  | (212) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})^{2}}\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\sim\mu\\
y,y^{\prime}\sim{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}^{(t)}\mid x\end{subarray}}\left[\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right].`$ |  | (213) |

Similarly, define

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi)`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}^{(t)}}[\beta\log\pi(y^{\prime}\mid x)-\beta\log\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y^{\prime}\mid x)]`$ |  | (214) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})^{2}}\sum_{(x,y,y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{D}^{(t)}}\left[\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]`$ |  | (215) |

where $`\mathcal{D}^{(t)}`$ is the dataset defined in iteration $`t`$ of
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
By
[Section J.2.1](#A10.SS2.SSS1.Px1 "KL-regularized policy optimization ‣ J.2.1 Background ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
we have $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\in\Pi`$, so
$`\inf_{\pi\in\Pi}\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi)\leq 0`$. Moreover by
definition, $`\pi^{(t)}\in\argmin_{\pi\in\Pi}\widehat{L}^{(t)}`$.

Let $`\Psi`$ be an $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}`$-net over $`\Pi`$, of
size $`\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})`$. Fix any
$`\pi\in\Psi`$ and $`2\leq t\leq T`$, and define increments
$`X_{i}:=\widehat{L}^{(i)}(\pi)-\widehat{L}^{(i-1)}(\pi)`$ for
$`2\leq i\leq t`$, with the notation $`\widehat{L}^{(1)}(\pi):=0`$ so
that $`\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi)=\sum_{i=2}^{t}X_{i}`$. Let
$`\mathcal{F}_{i}`$ be the filtration induced by $`\mathcal{D}^{(i)}`$
and define $`\gamma_{i}:=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]`$.
Observe that $`(t-1)L^{(t)}(\pi)=\sum_{i=2}^{t}\gamma_{i}`$. For any
$`i`$, note that we can write $`X_{i}=Y_{i}+Z_{i}`$ where
$`Y_{i}\in[-V_{\mathsf{max}},V_{\mathsf{max}}]`$ and
$`Z_{i}\in[0,\beta/\alpha]`$. By
[Section F.2](#A6.SS2 "F.2 Technical Tools ‣ Appendix F Preliminaries ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
it holds with probability at least $`1-\rho/(2|\Pi|T)`$

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{i=2}^{t}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\lesssim\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)+\sum_{i=2}^{t}Z_{i}.
``` |  | (216) |

By Azuma-Hoeffding, it holds with probability at least
$`1-\rho/(2|\Pi|T)`$ that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{i=2}^{t}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\lesssim V_{\mathsf{max}}\sqrt{T\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)}+\sum_{i=2}^{t}Y_{i}.
``` |  | (217) |

Hence, with probability at least $`1-\rho/(|\Psi|T)`$ we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(t-1)L^{(t)}(\pi)\lesssim\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)+V_{\mathsf{max}}\sqrt{T\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)}+\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi).
``` |  | (218) |

With probability at least $`1-\rho`$ this bound holds for all
$`\pi\in\Psi`$ and $`2\leq t\leq T`$. Henceforth condition on this
event. Fix any $`\pi\in\Pi`$ and $`2\leq t\leq T`$. Since $`\Psi`$ is an
$`\epsilon`$-net for $`\Pi`$, we see by definition of $`L^{(t)}`$ that
there is some $`\pi^{\prime}\in\Psi`$ such that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|L^{(t)}(\pi)-L^{(t)}(\pi^{\prime})|\lesssim\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})^{2}}\cdot\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})\leq\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\left(1+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})}\right)
``` |  | (219) |

and similarly

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
|\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi)-\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi^{\prime})|\lesssim(t-1)\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\left(1+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})}\right).
``` |  | (220) |

It follows that, for all $`2\leq t\leq T`$, since
$`\widehat{L}^{(t)}(\pi^{(t)})\leq 0`$, we get

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(t-1)L^{(t)}(\pi^{(t)})\lesssim\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)+V_{\mathsf{max}}\sqrt{T\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)}+\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}T\left(1+\frac{\beta}{\alpha(V_{\mathsf{max}}+R_{\mathsf{max}})}\right).
``` |  | (221) |

Hence,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}J_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-J_{\beta}(\pi^{(t)})`$ |  | (222) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\alpha}{\beta}(R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}})^{2}\cdot\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)+\frac{\beta}{\alpha T}+\frac{V_{\mathsf{max}}}{T}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=2}^{T}L^{(t)}(\pi^{(t)})`$ |  | (223) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim(R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}})\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)}{T}}+\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\sqrt{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}`$ |  | (224) |

by taking

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\alpha:=\frac{\beta}{R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(2|\Psi|T/\rho)}{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}}.
``` |  | (225) |

Since the output $`\widehat{\pi}`$ of
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
satisfies $`\widehat{\pi}\in\argmax_{t\in[T]}J_{\beta}(\pi^{(t)})`$, the
claimed bound on
$`J_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-J_{\beta}(\widehat{\pi})`$ is
immediate. Finally, observe that by definition of
$`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle J_{\beta}(\pi^{\star}_{\beta})-J_{\beta}(\widehat{\pi})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\E_{(x,y)\sim\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}\left[r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}\right]-\E_{(x,y)\sim\widehat{\pi}}\left[r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\widehat{\pi}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}\right]`$ |  | (226) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\E_{(x,y)\sim\pi^{\star}_{\beta}}\left[r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}\right]-\E_{(x,y)\sim\widehat{\pi}}\left[r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}\right]`$ |  | (227) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\E_{(x,y)\sim\widehat{\pi}}\left[\beta\log\frac{\widehat{\pi}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)}\right]`$ |  | (228) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\beta\log\E_{(x,y)\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}}[\exp(r(x,y))]-\beta\log\E_{(x,y)\sim\pi_{\texttt{base}}}[\exp(r(x,y))]+\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right)`$ |  | (229) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right).`$ |  | (230) |

This completes the proof. ∎

##### J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening

We now prove
[Section J.2.3](#A10.SS2.SSS3 "J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
the formal statement of
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px1 "Main result ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
which applies XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening. This result is a
straightforward corollary of
[Section J.2.2](#A10.SS2.SSS2 "J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
with the reward function
$`r_{\texttt{self}}(x,y):=\log\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)`$, together
with the observation that low KL-regularized regret implies sharpness
(under
[Section 4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")).

{theorem}

\[Sharpening via active exploration\] There are absolute constants
$`c_{\mathrm{\ref{thm:xpo-sharpening-apx}}},C_{\mathrm{\ref{thm:xpo-sharpening-apx}}}>0`$
so that the following holds. Let
$`\epsilon,\delta,\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}},\rho,\beta\in(0,1)`$ and
$`T\in\mathbb{N}`$ be given. For base model $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$,
define reward function $`r(x,y):=\log\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)`$. Let
$`R_{\mathsf{max}}\geq 1+\max_{x,y}\log\frac{1}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}`$.
Suppose that $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$ satisfies
[Section 4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
with parameter $`\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}`$, that
$`\beta^{-1}\geq 2\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}^{-1}\log(2|\mathcal{Y}|/\delta)`$,
and that there is $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\in(0,1)`$ so that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
T\geq C_{\mathrm{\ref{thm:xpo-sharpening-apx}}}\frac{R_{\mathsf{max}}^{2}\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)\log(2\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})T/\rho)}{\epsilon^{2}\delta^{2}\beta^{2}}
``` |  | (231) |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\leq c_{\mathrm{\ref{thm:xpo-sharpening-apx}}}\frac{\epsilon\delta}{\sqrt{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}}
``` |  | (232) |

where
$`\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi):=\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi,r,T,\beta,R_{\mathsf{max}}^{2};\pi_{\texttt{base}})`$.
Also suppose that $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\in\Pi`$ where
$`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)\propto\pi_{\texttt{base}}^{1+\beta^{-1}}(y\mid x)`$.

Then applying
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
with base model $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$, reward function $`r`$,
iteration count $`T`$, regularization $`\beta`$, and optimism parameter
$`\alpha:=\frac{\beta}{R_{\mathsf{max}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(2\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})T/\delta)}{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}}`$
yields a model $`\widehat{\pi}\in\Pi`$ such that with probability at
least $`1-\rho`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}[\widehat{\pi}(\bm{y}^{\star}(x)\mid x)<1-\delta]\leq\epsilon.
``` |  | (233) |

The total sample complexity is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
m=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{R_{\mathsf{max}}^{2}\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)\log(\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})/\rho)\log^{2}(\lvert\mathcal{Y}\rvert\delta^{-1})}{\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}^{2}\epsilon^{2}\delta^{2}}\right).
``` |  | (234) |

Proof of
[Section J.2.3](#A10.SS2.SSS3 "J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
By definition of $`r`$, we have $`|r(x,y)|\leq R_{\mathsf{max}}`$ for
all $`x,y`$. By assumption,
[Section J.2.1](#A10.SS2.SSS1.Px1 "KL-regularized policy optimization ‣ J.2.1 Background ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
is satisfied, and by definition of $`R_{\mathsf{max}}`$,
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2 "4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
is satisfied with parameter
$`V_{\mathsf{max}}:=\beta R_{\mathsf{max}}\leq R_{\mathsf{max}}`$. It
follows from
[Section J.2.2](#A10.SS2.SSS2 "J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
that with probability at least $`1-\rho`$, the output $`\widehat{\pi}`$
of
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
satisfies

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim(R_{\mathsf{max}}+V_{\mathsf{max}})\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)\log(2\mathcal{N}(\Pi,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})T/\rho)}{T}}`$ |  | (235) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad+\beta\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}\sqrt{\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi)T}.`$ |  | (236) |

By choice of $`T`$ and $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}`$, so long as
$`C_{\mathrm{\ref{thm:xpo-sharpening-apx}}}>0`$ is chosen to be a
sufficiently large constant and
$`c_{\mathrm{\ref{thm:xpo-sharpening-apx}}}>0`$ is chosen to be a
sufficiently small constant, we have
$`\beta D_{\mathsf{KL}}\left(\widehat{\pi}\,\|\,\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right)\leq\frac{1}{12}\beta\epsilon\delta`$,
so by e.g. Equation (16) of [Sason and Verdú (2016)](#bib.bib83),
$`D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\pi},\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right)\leq\epsilon\delta/(12)`$.

For any $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$ and
$`y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\bm{y}^{\star}(x)`$, by
[Section 4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
and definition of $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$ we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}\geq\frac{\max_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\max_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}\right)^{1+\beta^{-1}}`$ |  | (237) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq(1+\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}})^{1+\beta^{-1}}\geq e^{\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}/(2\beta)}\geq\frac{2|\mathcal{Y}|}{\delta}`$ |  | (238) |

where the final inequality is by the assumption on $`\beta`$ in the
theorem statement. Therefore

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(\bm{y}^{\star}(x)\mid x)\geq 1-\sum_{y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}\setminus\bm{y}^{\star}(x)}\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y^{\prime}\mid x)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{2}.
``` |  | (239) |

Now for any $`x`$, we can lower bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\pi}(\cdot\mid x),\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(\cdot\mid x)\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\left(\sqrt{1-\widehat{\pi}(\bm{y}^{\star}(x)\mid x)}-\sqrt{1-\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(\bm{y}^{\star}(x)\mid x)}\right)^{2}`$ |  | (240) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\cdot\mathbb{I}\{\widehat{\pi}(y^{\star}(x)\mid x)\leq 1-\delta\}.`$ |  | (241) |

Hence,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x\sim\mu}[\widehat{\pi}(\bm{y}^{\star}(x)\mid x)<1-\delta]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{12}{\delta}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mu}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\pi}(\cdot\mid x),\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(\cdot\mid x)\right)`$ |  | (242) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{12}{\delta}D^{2}_{\mathsf{H}}\left(\widehat{\pi},\pi^{\star}_{\beta}\right)`$ |  | (243) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\epsilon.`$ |  | (244) |

as claimed. ∎

##### J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models

In this section we apply
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px1 "Main result ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
to the class of linear softmax models, proving
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px2 "Example: Linearly parameterized models ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
This demonstrates that
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
can achieve an exponential improvement in sample complexity compared to
SFT-Sharpening.

{definition}

\[Linear softmax model\] Let $`d\in\mathbb{N}`$ be given, and let
$`\phi:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ be a feature map
with $`\lVert\phi(x,y)\rVert_{2}\leq 1`$ for all $`x,y`$. Let
$`\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})`$ be the uniform
model $`\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}(y\mid x):=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{Y}|}`$, and let
$`B\geq 1`$.¹⁵¹⁵ 15 We use the notation $`\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}`$ to
highlight the fact that $`\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}=\pi_{\theta}`$ for
$`\theta=0`$. We consider the linear softmax model class
$`\Pi_{\phi,B}:=\{\pi_{\theta}:\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}\leq B\}`$
where $`\pi_{\theta}:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta(\mathcal{Y})`$ is defined by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi_{\theta}(y\mid x)\propto\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}(y\mid x)\exp(\langle\phi(x,y),\theta\rangle).
``` |  | (245) |

{theorem}

\[Restatement of
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px2 "Example: Linearly parameterized models ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")\]
Let $`\epsilon,\delta,\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}},\rho\in(0,1)`$ be given.
Suppose that $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}=\pi_{\theta^{\star}}\in\Pi_{\phi,B}`$
for some $`\theta^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ with
$`\lVert\theta^{\star}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}B}{3\log(2|\mathcal{Y}|/\delta)}`$.
Also, suppose that $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$ satisfies
[Section 4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
with parameter $`\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}`$. Then
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
with base model $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$, reward function
$`r(x,y):=\log\pi_{\texttt{base}}(x,y)`$, regularization parameter
$`\beta:=\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}/(2\log(2|\mathcal{Y}|/\delta))`$, and
optimism parameter
$`\alpha(T)\propto\frac{\beta}{B+\log(|\mathcal{Y}|)}\sqrt{\frac{d\log(BdT/(\epsilon\delta))+\log(T/\rho)}{dT\log(T)}}`$
returns an $`(\epsilon,\delta)`$-sharpened model with probability at
least $`1-\rho`$, and has sample complexity

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
m=\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon^{-1},\delta^{-1},\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}^{-1},d,B,\log(|\mathcal{Y}|/\rho)).
``` |  | (246) |

Before proving the result, we unpack the conditions.
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
requires the base model $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$ to lie in the model
class and also satisfy the margin condition
([Section 4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")).
For any constant $`\epsilon,\delta>0`$, the sharpening algorithm then
succeeds with sample complexity
$`\mathrm{poly}(d,\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}^{-1},B,\log(|\mathcal{Y}|))`$.
These conditions are non-vacuous; in fact, there are fairly natural
examples for which non-exploratory algorithm such as SFT-Sharpening
require sample complexity $`\exp(\Omega(d))`$, whereas all of the above
parameters are $`\mathrm{poly}(d)`$. The following is one such example.

{example}

\[Separation between RLHF-Sharpening and SFT-Sharpening \] Set
$`\mathcal{X}=\{x\}`$ and let $`\mathcal{Y}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ be a
$`1/4`$-packing of the unit sphere in $`\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ of cardinality
$`\exp(\Theta(d))`$. Define
$`\phi:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ by
$`\phi(x,y):=y`$, and let $`B=Cd\log d`$ for an absolute constant
$`C>0`$. Fix any $`y^{\star}\in\mathcal{Y}`$ and define
$`\pi_{\texttt{base}}:=\pi_{\theta^{\star}}\in\Pi_{\phi,B}`$ by
$`\theta^{\star}:=y^{\star}`$. Then for any $`y\neq y^{\star}`$, we have
$`\langle y,y^{\star}\rangle\leq 1-\Omega(1)`$, so

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\star}\mid x)}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)}=\exp(\langle y^{\star}-y,y^{\star}\rangle)=\exp(\Omega(1))=1+\Omega(1).
``` |  | (247) |

Thus, $`\pi_{\texttt{base}}`$ satisfies
[Section 4.2](#S4.SS2 "4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
with $`\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}=\Omega(1)`$. Moreover,
$`\lVert\theta^{\star}\rVert_{2}=1\leq\frac{\gamma_{\mathsf{margin}}B}{3\log(2|\mathcal{Y}|/\delta)}`$
for any $`\delta=1/\mathrm{poly}(d)`$, so long as $`C`$ is a
sufficiently large constant. It follows from
[Section 4.2.2](#S4.SS2.SSS2.Px2 "Example: Linearly parameterized models ‣ 4.2.2 Benefits of Exploration ‣ 4.2 Analysis of RLHF-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
that
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In J.2.2 Guarantees for KL-regularized policy optimization with XPO ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
computes an $`(\epsilon,\delta)`$-sharpened model with sample complexity
$`\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon^{-1},\delta^{-1},d)`$. However, since
$`\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\star}\mid x)\leq\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)\cdot\exp(2)`$
for all $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$, it is clear that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
C_{\texttt{cov}}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(\bm{y}^{\star}(x)\mid x)}\right]=\frac{1}{\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y^{\star}\mid x)}=\Omega(|\mathcal{Y}|)=\exp(\Omega(d)).
``` |  | (248) |

Thus, the sample complexity guarantee for SFT-Sharpening in
[Section 4.1](#S4.SS1 "4.1 Analysis of SFT-Sharpening ‣ 4 Analysis of Sharpening Algorithms ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
will incur *exponential* dependence on $`d`$ in the sample complexity.
It is straightforward to check that this dependence is real for
SFT-Sharpening, and not just an artifact of the analysis, since the
model that SFT-Sharpening is trying to learn (via MLE) will itself not
be sharp in this example, unless $`\exp(\Omega(d))`$ samples are drawn
per prompt.

We now proceed to the proof of
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
which requires the following bounds on the covering number and the
Sequential Extrapolation Coefficient of $`\Pi_{\phi,B}`$.

{lemma}

Let $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}>0`$. Then $`\Pi_{\phi,B}`$ has an
$`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}`$-net of size
$`(6B/\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})^{d}`$.

Proof of
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
By a standard packing argument, there is a set
$`\{\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{N}\}`$ of size
$`(6B/\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})^{d}`$ such that for every
$`\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ with $`\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}\leq B`$ there
is some $`i\in[N]`$ with
$`\lVert\theta_{i}-\theta\rVert_{2}\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2`$. Now
for any $`x\in\mathcal{X}`$ and $`y\in\mathcal{Y}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y\mid x)}{\pi_{\theta_{i}}(y\mid x)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\log\frac{\exp(\langle\phi(x,y),\theta\rangle)}{\exp(\langle\phi(x,y),\theta_{i}\rangle)}+\log\frac{\E_{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\sim\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}}\exp(\langle\phi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),\theta_{i}\rangle)}{\E_{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\sim\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}}\exp(\langle\phi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),\theta\rangle)}`$ |  | (249) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\langle\phi(x,y),\theta-\theta_{i}\rangle+\log\frac{\E_{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\sim\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}}\left[\exp(\langle\phi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),\theta\rangle)\exp(\langle\phi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),\theta_{i}-\theta\rangle)\right]}{\E_{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\sim\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}}\exp(\langle\phi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),\theta\rangle)}.`$ |  | (250) |

The first term is bounded by $`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2`$ in
magnitude. In the second term, we have
$`\exp(\langle\phi(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),\theta_{i}-\theta\rangle)\in[\exp(-\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2),\exp(\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2)]`$,
so the ratio of expectations lies in
$`[\exp(-\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2),\exp(\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2)]`$
as well, and so the log-ratio lies in
$`[-\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2,\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}/2]`$. In all, we
get
$`\left|\log\frac{\pi_{\theta}(y|x)}{\pi_{\theta_{i}}(y\mid x)}\right|\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}`$.
Thus, $`\{\pi_{\theta_{1}},\dots,\pi_{\theta_{N}}\}`$ is an
$`\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}}`$-net for $`\Pi`$. ∎

{lemma}

Let
$`r:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to[-R_{\mathsf{max}},R_{\mathsf{max}}]`$
be a reward function and let $`T\in\mathbb{N}`$ and $`\beta>0`$. If
$`\lambda\geq 4\beta^{2}B^{2}+R_{\mathsf{max}}^{2}`$ then for any
$`\pi^{\star}\in\Pi_{\phi,B}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi_{\phi,B},r,T,\beta,\lambda;\pi^{\star})\lesssim d\log(T+1).
``` |  | (251) |

Proof of
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
Fix $`\pi^{(1)},\dots,\pi^{(T)}\in\Pi_{\phi,B}.`$ By definition, there
are some $`\theta^{(1)},\dots,\theta^{(T)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ with
$`\lVert\theta^{(t)}\rVert_{2}\leq B`$ and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)\propto\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}(y\mid x)\exp(\langle\phi(x,y),\theta^{(t)}\rangle)
``` |  | (252) |

for all $`t\in[T]`$ and $`(x,y)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}`$.
Similarly, there is some $`\theta^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ with
$`\lVert\theta^{\star}\rVert_{2}\leq B`$ and
$`\pi^{\star}(y\mid x)\propto\pi_{\mathsf{zero}}(y\mid x)\exp(\langle\phi(x,y),\theta^{\star}\rangle)`$.

Define
$`\widetilde{\phi}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+1}`$ by
$`\widetilde{\phi}(x,y):=[\phi(x,y),\frac{r(x,y)}{R_{\mathsf{max}}}]`$
and define
$`\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}:=[\beta(\theta^{(t)}-\theta^{\star}),-R_{\mathsf{max}}]`$.
Then for any $`t\in[T]`$ we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}^{(t)}\left[\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right]^{2}}{\lambda\lor\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}^{(i)}\left[\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]}`$ |  | (253) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}^{(t)}\left[\langle\widetilde{\phi}(x,y)-\widetilde{\phi}(x,y^{\prime}),\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}\rangle\right]^{2}}{\lambda\lor\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}^{(i)}\left[\left(\langle\widetilde{\phi}(x,y)-\widetilde{\phi}(x,y^{\prime}),\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}\rangle\right)^{2}\right]}`$ |  | (254) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(t)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}{\lambda\lor\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(i)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}`$ |  | (255) |

where for each $`i\in[T]`$ we have defined
$`\Sigma^{(i)}:=\mathbb{E}^{(i)}\left[(\widetilde{\phi}(x,y)-\widetilde{\phi}(x,y^{\prime}))(\widetilde{\phi}(x,y)-\widetilde{\phi}(x,y^{\prime}))^{\top}\right]`$.
Observe that
$`\lVert\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 4\beta^{2}B^{2}+R_{\mathsf{max}}^{2}\leq\lambda`$
by assumption on $`\lambda`$. Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(t)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}{\lambda\lor\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(i)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(t)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}{\lambda+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(i)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}`$ |  | (256) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\Sigma^{(t)}\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}{(\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)\widetilde{\theta}^{(t)}}`$ |  | (257) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left(\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma^{(t)}\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)^{-1/2}\right)`$ |  | (258) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\Tr\left(\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)^{-1/2}\Sigma^{(t)}\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)^{-1/2}\right)`$ |  | (259) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\Tr\left(\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\Sigma^{(t)}\right).`$ |  | (260) |

Observe that
$`\Tr(\Sigma^{(t)})\leq\max_{x,y}\lVert\widetilde{\phi}(x,y)\rVert_{2}^{2}\lesssim 1`$.
Hence by
[Section F.2](#A6.SS2 "F.2 Technical Tools ‣ Appendix F Preliminaries ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathbb{E}^{(t)}\left[\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right]^{2}}{\lambda\lor\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}^{(i)}\left[\left(\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y\mid x)}-r(x,y)-\beta\log\frac{\pi^{(t)}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}{\pi^{\star}(y^{\prime}\mid x)}+r(x,y^{\prime})\right)^{2}\right]}`$ |  | (261) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Tr\left(\left(I_{d}+\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\Sigma^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\Sigma^{(t)}\right)`$ |  | (262) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lesssim d\log(T+1).`$ |  | (263) |

Since $`\pi^{(1)},\dots,\pi^{(T)}\in\Pi`$ were arbitrary, this completes
the proof. ∎

The proof is now immediate from
[Section J.2.3](#A10.SS2.SSS3 "J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
and the above lemmas.

Proof of
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism").
By the assumption on $`\theta^{\star}`$ and choice of $`\beta`$, the
model $`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}`$ defined by
$`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}(y\mid x)\propto\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)^{1+\beta^{-1}}`$
satisfies
$`\pi^{\star}_{\beta}=\pi_{(1+\beta^{-1})\theta^{\star}}\in\Pi_{\phi,B}`$.
By
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
we have
$`\mathcal{N}(\Pi_{\phi,B},\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})\leq(6B/\epsilon_{\mathsf{disc}})^{d}`$.
Take
$`R_{\mathsf{max}}:=\sqrt{4\beta^{2}B^{2}+(2B+\log|\mathcal{Y}|)^{2}}`$.
We know that $`r(x,y):=\log\pi_{\texttt{base}}(y\mid x)`$ satisfies
$`|r(x,y)|\leq 2B+\log|\mathcal{Y}|`$ for all $`x,y`$. By
[Section J.2.4](#A10.SS2.SSS4 "J.2.4 Application: linear softmax models ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism"),
we therefore get that
$`\mathsf{SEC}(\Pi_{\phi,B},r,T,\beta,R_{\mathsf{max}}^{2};\pi_{\texttt{base}})\lesssim d\log(T+1)`$.
Substituting these bounds into
[Section J.2.3](#A10.SS2.SSS3 "J.2.3 Applying XPO to maximum-likelihood sharpening ‣ J.2 Proof of and ‣ Appendix J Proofs from ‣ Part II Proofs ‣ Self-Improvement in Language Models:The Sharpening Mechanism")
yields the claimed result. ∎
````
